## Video 92: Today, my art bought me groceries

- **Video ID:** yuAcn1GbhLk
- **URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yuAcn1GbhLk
- **Published:** Unknown
- **Duration:** 1:06
- **Views:** 19,767
- **Description:** N/A...

### Transcript

Today my art bought me food to eat.
Today my art bought me groceries. Today
my art let me buy peanut butter without
having an aneurysm. And as an artist
that is all I have ever wanted. Some
people tell me that what I make isn't
art. Some people tell me that I'm not an
artist or if I am, I'm a bad one, an
undeserving one. And those people might
be right, but those people aren't buying
me groceries, so who gives a I
would spend nights lying awake imagining
what my life would be like without art.
A joyless life, one I would rather not
live. I would set mental dates for
myself to end myself. How many days I
could go living in fear and worry and
artlessness. And I'm still very afraid
and still worrying. But I am not artless
because as long as I am alive, I can
make art. And as long as I can make art,
I can stay alive. I was prepared to
starve for my art. I have starved for my
art. I have mentally calculated the
price of feeling full and feeling
nourished and picked the feeling of
fullness over the nourishment. But today
I feel full in my stomach but in my
heart as well because today my art
bought me groceries.

## Video 107: A Christmas Story

- **Video ID:** fzYys2PhNFI

- **Duration:** 4:01

This was when Liam was too afraid to show his face online hehehe this was also his story what a beautiful man I swear...

### Transcript

Oh, hello. I was just reading one of my
favorite Christmas stories. Would you
like to hear it? Why don't you grab a
warm cup of cocoa and relax. It should be
a short tale. I want to show you
something, said the voice
Billy rubbed his eyes at the noise and
looked around the living room. The whole
place was surrounded in yellow and blue.
Tinsel stuck toward the wall, and the
ceiling and beneath the green plastic
wreath hung above the fireplace three
fluffy red stockings dangled from the
mantle. The Christmas tree his family
kept in the garage all year was now
fixed on a carpet in the center of the
room sparkling with stars and lightbulbs
and standing in front of it with a smile
on his face and a glimmer in his eyes
was the jolly man himself, his big nose
frostbitten red, his cheeks rosy, his big
coat buttoned up tight and cozy, "wait,"
Billy said. Something felt off, not like
it should. He was sleepy and his stomach
was hurting from all he had eaten before bed.
"You're here already?" Billy said and
looked over his shoulder to the digital
clock on the coffee table
11:28 it read. Behind the tree the
curtains were drawn closed. "Well I had to
wait and see that your parents were
asleep didn't I?"
Santa spoke
Red mitts brushed crumbs off his beard
and Billy felt weird. He was nine now and
it seemed the older he got the slower
the holidays were to get here and the
quicker they were to leave but here he
was standing in a room with decorations
and Santa Claus and nowhere to be but
now and still he felt weird, not in this
stomach but in his head. It was like when
he and his dad flew to Australia, when
Billy got on the plane it was snowy and
cold and when he got off the air was dry
and hot. That was weird getting used to.
He looked at the stockings again. "Come on, Billy,"
Santa said. "Where's your holiday spirit?"
Of course when he went to sleep he
expected Christmas but didn't expect it
to come so fast. "Don't you want to see
what it's like where I live?"
Santa said. "Where we have Christmas
forever?" Beneath this frosted bushy
eyebrows Santa's eyes didn't glimmer. He took a
step towards Billy. Billy stepped back, somewhere, the floor cracked.
"Come outside," he said, "your spirit is going to get cold. Come outside, now!" Billy fled. He didn't
want to be the same room as Santa anymore.
His feet left the carpet and slipped on the floor.
He could hear the thump of Santa's footsteps coming after him. In the dim
light he could see the hallway. Billy caught himself on the coffee table and pushed himself up all the way.
And ran forward, the hallway wasn't far, he turned the corner, he slammed into something hard, he
fell backwards, he knocked his head. "What are you doing?!" someone said.
Billy squinted his
eyes looked up at his dad. "I can't
believe you're awake," he said grabbing
Billy's hand. "Next year, no more Halloween
candy before bed.
If it has you running around
the house like this," he said. "Come on,
let's both get back to sleep."
Billy's dad boy stood him up and turned
to leave. From over his shoulder Billy
looked back at the scene. In the living
room there were no decorations at all.

## Video 110: Why I Make Art — Spoken Word

- **Video ID:** nvfAqKXgl0A
- **URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nvfAqKXgl0A
- **Published:** Unknown
- **Duration:** 2:06
- **Views:** 4,018
- **Description:** Hope this clears things up...

### Transcript

A long time ago I found myself in a cave
I didn't know until it was too late. I
had got there so slow I couldn't find my
way back, and no matter which way I
looked around me I just saw black. But
that wasn't the main thing. The main thing
was that it was lonely. The only thing I
really wanted more than light was
company. It was the loneliness that was
the worst part. The darkness was
manageable. Stay in the cave long enough
and you learned to navigate. Stay in the
cave long enough in your eyes adjust.
Stay in the cave long enough and you
start to think the cave is your friend.
give up trying to find a way out. You
stop wanting light it would hurt your
eyes, you stop thinking about the fight
you need to rise out of the cave, and so
dark becomes all you know. And it's not
so bad in the cave, it's damp and cramped
but something about the darkness put me
in a dull trance. And when I snap out of
it I would ask myself how do I get out
of the cave? And when I didn't have the energy or
the confidence to ask myself that
question sometimes all I can do is
scream. Because screaming felt like it
said more than the words, how do I get
out, how do I get out, how do I get out, no
one heard me so I screamed how do I get
out and no one heard me so I screamed
even louder, I yelled into the cave until
my voice was sore and when I could I
would scream some more and then after
years of screaming I heard someone
scream back. And in that moment I
realized I wasn't the only one who could
only see black. Now sometimes I hear
screams from people like me lost in the
cave trying to find their way out. When
they hear me they scream and when I hear
them I scream back. That's all I can do I
just scream back. But the scream is more
meaningful than words, the scream speaks
in metaphors, the scream is more English
than English, I'm screaming right now.
I've tried to tell people I'm in a cave
but they don't get it until I scream and
when I scream nothing matters but the
sound coming out of my mouth.
Nothing is more cathartic than a scream,
nothing says more than a scream there's
no better way to say what can't be said.
And that's why I make art

---

I don't know who I am.

discovery seems really difficult to me.

I

artificially.

## Video 120: Don't Fall In Love With Depression

- **Published:** Unknown
- **Duration:** 1:27

~words~
Do not make friends with your depression
Do not shake hands with......

### Transcript

I haven't learned a lot from being
depressed, but I have learned this. Do
not make friends with your depression.
Do not shake hands with your depression.
Do not subsist off a diet of depression.
You are more than someone who is sick.
You are more than someone who is alone.
You're not alone. Hi. Do not fall in
love with your depression. Love is born
out of proximity. We fall in love with
those who are close to us, who have been
with us for a long time. We fall in love
not out of love but out of convenience.
Make an exception for depression.
Depression has been with you a long
time, but it does not love you. Don't
love it. Do not love your abuser just
because they make you feel less alone.
Do not develop Stockholm syndrome for
mental illness. Do not want this. Do not
want to be depressed. Do not want to
love what kills you every day. Do not
hold it so close that when someone tries
to pry it away, you feel you yourself
are being attacked. They're trying to
help you. But you have to help yourself.
And you have to want to help yourself.
Depression is not your friend.
Depression is not your identity.
Depression is a parasite fat with your
blood. Depression is not something that
you should love. So when you say I am
depressed, just remember you're more
than that.
